But the test `test_render_marks_last_row_as_total` is separate.\n\nLet's look at the test file `test_export.py`:\n\n```python\nfrom export import export_csv\nfrom render import render_rows\nfrom totals import column_totals\n\ndef test_export_without_totals():\n    assert export_csv([[1, 2], [3, 4]]) == \"1,2\\n3,4\"\n\ndef test_column_totals_ignores_text_cells():\n    assert column_totals([[\"a\", 1], [\"b\", 2]]) == [\"\", 3]\n\ndef test_render_marks_last_row_as_total():\n    assert render_rows([[1, 2], [3, 4]], mark_last=True) == [\"1,2\", \"TOTAL,3,4\"]\n\ndef test_export_appends_totals_row():\n    assert export_csv([[1, 2], [3, 4]], totals=True) == \"1,2\\n3,4\\nTOTAL,4,6\"\n```\n\nSo `render_rows` is expected to take `mark_last` argument. `export_csv` uses `render_rows` without `mark_last` (presumably). The `test_export_appends_totals_row` expects `export_csv` to produce a string with a totals row appended. It doesn't involve `render_rows` marking the last row.\n\nThus, we need to satisfy both:\n- `render_rows` should accept `mark_last` and mark the last row.\n- `export_csv` should append a totals row using `column_totals` and call `render_rows` on the combined rows (without `mark_last`? or with? Probably without, because the totals row is already marked with \"TOTAL\" in the first column).\n\nLet's check the expected output of `test_export_appends_totals_row`: `\"1,2\\n3,4\\nTOTAL,4,6\"`. The last row is \"TOTAL,4,6\". This row has \"TOTAL\" as the first column, and then the totals. This is different from `render_rows` marking the last row as `TOTAL,3,4` (which would be on the second last row). So in `export_csv` when `totals=True`, we append a new row that is the totals, and we don't want `render_rows` to mark the previous last row as total. So we should call `render_rows` on `all_rows` without `mark_last`.\n\nThus, `export_csv` should be:\n\n```python\ndef export_csv(rows, totals=False):\n    if totals:\n        totals_row = [\"TOTAL\"] + [str(t) for t in column_totals(rows)]\n        all_rows = rows + [totals_row]\n        return \"\\n\".join(render_rows(all_rows))  # without mark_last\n    else:\n        return \"\\n\".join(render_rows(rows))\n```\n\nAnd `render_rows` should handle `mark_last` when called separately.\n\nSo we need to modify `render.py` to accept `mark_last` parameter.\n\nLet's edit `render.py`:\n\n```bash\nedit render.py with new content that handles mark_last\n```\n\nBut we need to know the exact old text to replace. Currently `render.py` contains:\n\n```python\ndef render_rows(rows):\n    return [\",\".join(str(v) for v in r) for r in rows]\n```\n\nWe can replace this with a function that takes `mark_last=False`:\n\n```python\ndef render_rows(rows, mark_last=False):\n    rendered = [\",\".join(str(v) for v in r) for r in rows]\n    if mark_last and rows:\n        # Get the last row's values\n        last_row_vals = rows[-1]\n        # Render it with \"TOTAL\" prepended\n        last_rendered = \"TOTAL,\" + \",\".join(str(v) for v in last_row_vals)\n        rendered[-1] = last_rendered\n    return rendered\n```\n\nBut wait, the test expects `render_rows([[1, 2], [3, 4]], mark_last=True)` to return `[\"1,2\", \"TOTAL,3,4\"]`. With the above, `last_rendered` would be `\"TOTAL,3,4\"`, and the previous row is `\"1,2\"`. So `[\"1,2\", \"TOTAL,3,4\"]` matches.\n\nHowever, note that `render_rows` is also called by `export_csv` without `mark_last`, so it should work as before.\n\nLet's try this edit.\n\nBut first, let's check if there are other issues.
